We undertake, of course, to do our part with regard to refugees, those in need of protection. Ladies and gentlemen, I think the problem is and was that we had to feel relatively alone with the entire process, except for Germany and Sweden. Therefore: It is not that we want to help, it is that we cannot tolerate illegal migration, but rather that we need regulated and solidarity-based migration across Europe. That's the process, that's the difference. [[I should like to congratulate Mr Weninger and Mr Strolz on his report.]] If you look at the interim results – and I think it is an intermediate result – it was necessary that we have made a call here, an interim solution, because in the final effect is what we have proposed, European consensus. Mr President Tusk recently defended Europe on Wednesday, in particular the Balkans, and praised the closing of the Balkan route as an expression of this illegal migration. We therefore feel supported, but not only supported, but we have also proposed this path. [[The President-in-Office of the Council shall take over the Presidency.]] The crucial question now, I believe, is: Together in Europe, we must ensure that the Balkan route remains closed. Even when pressure is built up, it must not come to an opening! Why? – Because otherwise exactly the same thing happens, which has been the case before, namely that the uncontrolled, which would be maintained in quantitatively non-controllable migration, inflow to Austria and Germany. That is why I believe it is right to thank the Macedonian government for making a contribution to its borders – which is not easy – to meet this very goal of a lasting solution to support this objective. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] But that's not enough on its own. Greece must also make its contribution and must not allow people to continue streaming to the border, because of this, of course, pressure is being built up, situations arise that we do not want. We are often told in this context that we have now exported the Austrian problem to Greece and that Greece would now stand alone. Ladies and gentlemen, that is not the case. We have received 100 000 people in our country and the asylum applications are being processed. Greece has not even reached 50 000 and is a country larger than Austria. That is why we must say, of course, that we are aware of the financial problems there, but look at what the European Union is doing: It provides financial assistance – it has already done this last year and has now announced €600 million – and, of course, humanitarian aid. We also want to do this, but I believe that it is quite reasonable for the country to stay there – in an EU country – if the supply is guaranteed, and have no right to go to Germany or elsewhere in accordance with its programme of desire – that is what I believe we must guarantee – and that this expectation of refugees: We only need to produce pressure at the border, and somewhere there is a solution!, then is actually justified in practice. There is no reason to escape from an EU country like Greece and no right to choose the best country. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, there are two things to do: One must also appeal, namely to the tractors – that will be difficult, I know – but above all to the activists, who, misunderstanded or intending to actually implement it, spread false information, because, with this illicit transition, they naturally endanger the very lives of those, in the rivers they actually want to protect. The accusation goes exactly in this direction, and I think it is also entitled. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, Germany must also be made clear from its silent role, and I think there are two possibilities: Either says Germany, we take up so many migrants and then take them to the country, whichever way they go – that's the one variant; then you can also serve expectations accordingly – or Germany says – that's the other variant –, we have also reached the border, we can't also absorb unlimited and absolutely. And then, when that is said, the expectation among the refugees will be another one. As I do with my opinion, you can see from the fact that most of the refugees do not accept offers, to Luxembourg, to Norway, to Portugal – precisely in expectation, there is at some point the savior word or the redeeming opening towards Germany and everything is as before. Ladies and gentlemen, this is necessary and we will also insist that a clear statement is made that there is either a certain quota, or a statement that one says that we can make out of certain green That's not the one either. There will also be certain resources and no longer. But of course we need to do more. The next step, Mr Bundeskanzler, will also be to call on the Council to ensure the escape routes, but also to secure the external borders. I do not think we can have the view that we are now making a deal with Turkey and Turkey will do everything on its own. On the contrary, we need NATO, we need Frontex with extended order to protect the external borders. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] This is not only a right, it is also a duty that we have agreed in the Schengen Treaty, and we also insist on this obligation on the part of Austria. That is, as far as the Turkey Treaty is concerned, we must not be pressured to surrender there. It is visa conditions and, in particular, in the event that problems arise, it is also a kind of revision clause to ensure that Europe's interests are respected accordingly. We know all that. Therefore, I think Austria has been given time with our initiative. You should see this so that you can't appreciate it high enough. Why? – Because in the final effect, if, as the EU had actually planned, we had waited until June, we would have faced an unregulated stream of people and could not do anything more. I think it is in the interest of refugees, in the interest of individual countries, to have orderly conditions and orderly access. It will therefore be important to achieve a reasonable outcome with Turkey tomorrow. To join in the expectation that everything would be solved would certainly be wrong. We've got an intermediate step. And before all the other steps are taken at the overall solidarity and European level, it is up to the policy of continuing the policies of national action. That is right, that is the requirement for all other measures to be taken. In this sense: A common line of Austria! In this sense: No other alternative! I hope you will support this path.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-03-16-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00117_d8e10697	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mrs. State Secretary! Mr State Secretary! I have to address the procedure again. In other words, I understand in principle that members of the government cannot be present all the time in a parliamentary debate here, as there are always situations in this regard. But if there is a statement by the Federal Government, by the Chancellor of the Federal Republic and by the Vice-Chancellor, you can do that, you can say what Mr Kickl, Mrs Korun, Mr Wöginger and I say, or not, but we are here together in the debate, not only listening to the statement, but discussing all that we may want to give to the Federal Government – and the two gentlemen are not here, then I really think this is a living and self-confident Parliament. [[I should like to congratulate the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats.]] The Federal Chancellor, I believe, has said quite right things at least in part. He said, on the one hand, that Turkey is a neighbor, but a more difficult one, and you have to talk to a neighbor. That's how I see it. I don't think it's going to work that we're going to come to solutions without neighbors. The second thing he said was that there must be no exchange of values between the European Union and Turkey. The only problem is that the plan currently on the table provides to some degree such an exchange of values. This plan can only work if we agree at European level that Turkey is on the one hand a safe third country and on the other hand a safe country of origin. If we do not, it will be difficult on the one hand that Greece will send all refugees back to Turkey, and on the other hand it will be difficult because many Kurds who have to suffer repression in Turkey, sometime when there are visa facilitations, will make their way and will seek asylum from us. There are currently three very important points in the plan, which is why this plan is, on the one hand, not in order and, on the other hand, morally reprehensible: The first point is that Turkey is not a safe third country; the second point is that mass movements, as they are planned, are not possible in any way after the refugee convention or after the human rights convention; and the third point, which we have already heard, is the current human rights situation in Turkey. A safe third country is a country in which refugees, if they get there, have the opportunity to apply for asylum proceedings and find protection under the Geneva Convention on Refugees. That is, they must have the opportunity to be able to find themselves decent, to live there for a while and to stay there. The requirement for them to be able to apply for asylum at all, to obtain a fair procedure for their requests for protection, and to be taken up and accommodated decently if they receive a positive asylum notice. It is currently regulated in the European reception directive in such a way that a safe third country is a state that has ratified the Geneva Refugee Convention without any regional reservation. Turkey does not have that, although Turkey has ratified the Geneva Convention on Refugees, it is with a reservation that only European citizens and European Union citizens can apply for asylum in the first place. In other words, once this is done, Turkey cannot be a safe third country. The second possibility that exists is that regional reservations are not the problem by type. 38 of the reception directive. However, it is also a matter of respecting the actual possibilities of the Geneva Convention on Refugees – that is, that I can at all apply for asylum and that the material guarantees are guaranteed at all. Here, too, the European Commission itself says that there are very massive, serious shortcomings. That is to say, Turkey cannot be a safe third country. The second essential point is the question of collective designations. I have already said that neither the European Convention on Human Rights nor the Convention on Refugees, nor the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the EU, nor the Treaties of Europe, nor does it go in any way in a rule of law to send collective people back to another country. [[Intrusion of the Abg. New builder.]] The right to asylum is an individual right. I must have the possibility that my own claims will also be taken into account, heard and examined accordingly, and only then will it be decided whether there is an asylum ground or not. If anyone is present, I must also have the opportunity to get asylum in the context. That means it won't work either. [[Abg. Lugar: Wrong!]] – It is right, Mr Lugar. I know you're reading the Geneva Refugee Convention, but just as you want it, that's always up to half and then you stop it. I'd like to give you some help, let's get together, let's go through the basics! [[Abg. Lugar: This is stupid! Don't be so depressing!]] - It's not depressing! Just because you're wrong in reading them and you don't understand them, that's not depressing. You can read them together with the comments once in peace. [[Congratulation with the NEOS and the Abg. Steinhauser.]] That means that this will not work, either, because collective rejections according to the various human rights documents are not possible. The last point concerns the human rights situation in Turkey: On the one hand, we can read the Commission's reports, on the other hand simply press the newspaper every day in the early days, and see that the Commission is talking about setbacks in democratic writing, democratic fundamental rights in Turkey, and there are steps in the fight against corruption. The Commission, as well as the Austrian judicial association, criticise the establishment of the judicial system in Turkey. We see that judges and prosecutors are under constant pressure. We note that there are massive setbacks in freedom of expression and assembly and that journalists are being detained and determined against them. All of this is happening – and this is the exciting one – although, as the European Union, we are investing €4.5 billion in Turkey between 2014 and 2020 only as a support for the further development of human rights, fundamental rights and democracy. There is no progress, but there is a massive step back. We see that we are investing money here, but the human rights situation in Turkey is getting worse. This whole plan, which is on the table, cannot work for three reasons: Turkey is not a safe third country, mass repatriation is not possible, and third, due to the human rights situation in Turkey. What we really would need – then we could end this egg dance that we are organizing with Turkey – is to finally create legal entry opportunities. It does not help if the Federal Chancellor, the Minister of the Interior and the Foreign Minister always want to announce it in full, saying that we need 1 900 people in Austria, reversed but legally, through resettlement programmes. That is to say, we need sensible resettlement measures so that we can get the tractors out of their hands. We must make it possible to give people legal opportunity to flee. We need to do this and not just talk about it, as the Federal Government is doing right now, because 1,900 people I personally think are extremely few. And there's no wonder that many are on their way and are just trying to get to Austria and Europe. That is to say, we must create legal opportunities for admission and we must see that we are changing this Turkey deal, as it is now on the table, as far as possible, so that we can look at the mirror in some way as European Union in accordance with our basic values.

The third point concerns the splitting of pensions. They propose a mandatory pension division, we stand in this area on the principle of freedom of choice. We currently have a splitting possibility, i.e. the contributions of men and women in a partnership or in a marriage are counted together, divided by two, and half of each account is paid out. This will also be done, and I believe that these are good family policy points, especially with regard to higher pensions for women in this area.

This is the monthly notification of the number of recognised refugees and eligible beneficiaries in unemployment statistics. ParlaMint-AT_2016-03-16-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00117_d8e15985	Madam President. Minister! High house! As a doctor, I support the FPÖ application for the merger of social security funds. – The fact that this is going on can be proven historically. Ladies and gentlemen! In 1890, shortly after the first Austrian social legislation, we had 3,000 health insurance funds in Austria. 3 000 health insurance companies with a population of about 40 million! That's a little bit of a calculation. If you break this down on today, you can see what that would mean. 1890: 3 000 health insurance funds, 1927: 150 health insurance funds, 2016: a total of 34, if you charge the health care institutions. If we're already with the historical, I'm allowed to put a little whip into the past, how our health insurance system ever came into being. This is always to be kept in mind. We have chosen the so-called Bismarck model for the health insurance and pension system. This is the end of the 19th century by Prince Bismarck. ==References====External links== This was, based on the future structures, on district structures, regional and professional structures, an insurance model that was dependent on the contributions paid by the insured, and which, of course, was built on a large number of young contributors and few old and sick. That's why this has worked very well in the beginning and for many decades. It must not be forgotten, however, that these systems have been constructed in terms of terms and actually in terms of state and are still in place. And that is why we still have self-management and these own regulations per health insurance fund today and that no democratic or almost democratic intervention is possible from the outside. I am always surprised that the SPÖ is so much in favour of something state-of-the-art, if you want to, and that there is no rethinking into modernity, into the third millennium. The counterpart to the Bismarck systems we have in Austria in the ambulatory field are the Beveridge systems, which also introduced a high-noble in England, which were then adopted by the Nordic countries. Lord Beveridge has the beginning of the 20th century. ==References== This is a purely tax, publicly funded system that does not have an insurance character. This gives rise to the two opposites. We live in Austria. We're living the compromise. We have now integrated both of them. Then: The stationary structures we have, the hospitals, are pure Beveridge systems with a bit of Bismarck inside. They are co-financed by the public's contributions, but in principle they are financed by the stationary structures through tax funds. In other words, we have found a mix in recent decades, also through the ASVG 1956 and smaller novels that we have made over and over again, we have basically a mix of Beveridge and Bismarck systems, i.e. from contribution systems and tax-financed systems. This has several advantages, but also serious disadvantages. As I said, The Bismarck systems are basically opaque, locked down and difficult to influence in the overall democratic situation. This is the so-called self-management that we are quite proud of. But this one has the disadvantage of not being able to control it marginally through the parliamentary procedure or through the individual citizen. The citizen has no choice, he is committed to the respective insurance; depending on the place of residence, he must accept his insurance according to his professional activity. I believe that we should consider whether we should introduce innovations in the sense of homogenization. The topic of administrative costs: Administrative costs are not 2 per cent or 4 per cent or whatever is communicated here. It's much more! To this end, you can read the studies of Czypionka and Pichlbauer: It is at least 7 to 9 percent, depending on the study, but at least it is significantly more than the always communicated 2 to 4 percent administrative costs. It's almost 10 percent! Canada – which has a Beveridge system – has proven 2 percent administrative costs in comparison. I believe that all this should lead us to think about homogenisation and that we are taking up the Leitel proposal with the three major administrative bodies: the employees, the officials and the self-employed, that we make three bodies out of the 34 we now have, and that health insurance takes over all costs, not only the costs for the established doctors, but also very probably the costs for the hospitals. We are always talking about financing from a single source, we should also do so by requiring health insurance to take over all health costs per patient. [[Abg. Fekter: That's not going to happen!]] This would result in much better control options, significantly more transparency and much more efficiency in the system. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-03-16-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00117_d8e16643	Mr President. Minister! Mr State Secretary! Required minimum noise for electric vehicles: In the urban area, the registration of electric vehicles has doubled in recent years. An acoustic perception of these vehicles is almost non-existent in urban traffic. The daily traffic situation also includes particularly vulnerable persons, such as children, visually impaired and hearing disabled persons, in which the principle of trust is sometimes suspended. The technology is so advanced that acoustic modules are offered for a significant improvement in visibility. It should be noted that acoustic warnings corresponding to heavy traffic are required by law and are in use, for example, the vehicle will be moved backward. Therefore, it is up to the legislator to establish a legal basis to make all electric vehicles authorised for public transport mandatory with a corresponding warning acoustic device. A sound of easier perceptibility shall be adjusted to or exceed the conditions of daily traffic, such as minimum or maximum speed, as well as the usual noise level. For urban transport, registration numbers of electric vehicles will continue to increase, so that the safety of legislators is required. I would like to vote in favour of the request. Thank you.

The conditions under which Chinese steel is produced, ladies and gentlemen, are not comparable to labour law or environmental standards in Europe or worldwide. This is different when it comes to equity – therefore: A risky entrepreneur prefers equity, and that's a good thing. It is risk capital: In the case of risk capital, the State likes to interfere because it says investors need to be protected. Therefore, one has gone in the direction of demanding prospectuses. We have gone very far, so that this example of Staudinger has completely passed away from reality. At some point, the legislator recognized this and passed the Alternative Financing Act, which is in reality a prospectus reduction law. The threshold has simply been lowered. Now, of course, one can say that there is a contradiction that the more and those will be less protected. It is actually a deregulation law, it is a law that goes towards more entrepreneurship, towards more risk capital and towards more market economy. And to such a one. Law and such an invitation to the government to make this law popular can only be said Yes. Thank you.

That means if I travel to my guests offer, for example, combined with a mountain guide or similar offers, then as a company I need a travel agency license which constitutes a further commercial permit. That is why it was my primary concern to try to pull the poison teeth at the national level in the area of regulation. I am pleased that I have an ally in my colleague Gabriel Obernosterbeim Coalitionspartner and we have found a common line and have been able to make this request. We have spent essential things compared to the original directive approach. For the first time, State Secretary Mahrer has been able to successfully participate in various areas with his negotiating team in Brussels. For example: A trip is now considered a package trip only if the additional service is more than 25 percent, so two small services do not automatically carry out a package trip with themselves and subsequently booked on-site also do not trigger a package trip. Damage for lost holiday delights can only be claimed if there is a significant restriction. Secondly, I immediately worked to ensure that combination offers can be maintained, and that the second necessary business authorisation does not create red tape and even higher costs, because this is central to hoteliers. The hoteliers live on additional offers, which is the key to success for many businesses. This is not a question of making more revenue, but simply and grippingly about positioning itself on the market. Ladies and gentlemen, we must be aware of one thing: Austria is a country that benefits sustainablely from tourism. The Austrian brand, the Austrian image in Germany and abroad is strongly influenced by our hospitality and tourist diversity. That it remains so, we have a part of the responsibility here in the National Council. I'm glad most of them pull the same line. In conclusion, however, I do not want to save with criticism. I would have expected more cooperation on the part of the Chamber of Commerce. My intention is and was to achieve an unbureaucratic, free implementation of the directive, it has become a request for cost-effective implementation due to the opposition of the board, which I do not fully understand. Then: If businesses already pay €676 million per year to the Chamber of Commerce, you should conclude that enough is actually enough in such a scheme as a chamber itself. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Obernosterer.]] After all, the Chamber is also our representative, so our friend, if you want to. For me, cost-effective – even if it is now cost-effective – still implies the chance of a free variant. I wonder if it would not be possible to make it free of charge, because at least € 67 million was spent on the company pensions of the ex-carriers last year. Maybe you could use the money a little different. Ladies and gentlemen! Let's take it on and keep tourism fit for the future! I think that's our common task. At this point I am already thanking myself for the broad support of my request. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-03-16-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00117_d8e21357	Next speaker: Mr. Sieber. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-03-16-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00117_d8e21378	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen. Our hoteliers were not wrong to worry that the European Union's 2015 package travel directive overly burdened the local accommodation establishments. The above directive provides that when booking rooms in combination with other services, if they exceed the threshold of 25 percent of the total price, this booking becomes a package trip. However, it may offer such a package trip under Austrian law only those who are also in possession of a travel agency license. This would not only involve additional costs, such as the double chamber housing, but also more bureaucratic expenditure. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Especially in winter tourism, additional services such as ski passes or the rental of ski equipment are often offered, and also for health tourism, where wellness treatments are offered, this would lead to problems. That is why this exemption is a negotiation success of State Secretary Mahrer up to the 25 percent threshold. In addition to the obligations arising from Community law and which can no longer be removed nationally, it is necessary to extend the hospitality sector in order to preserve the establishments from the obligation of an additional travel agency licence and a double chamber cover. Our State Secretary, Mahrer, has already negotiated well at EU level, has been able to step up the original draft and has made a strong commitment to practical implementation. In the interests of the competitiveness of the local hotel industry with its high-quality range of services, I am convinced that State Secretary Mahrer will insist on a practical application of the directive. We agree with this motion for a resolution.
